The EngineCore process is sleeping (State: S). It has 39 threads. VmRSS is 1057160 kB (~1GB). The process seems to be waiting for something. This is unusual - normally weight loading should be actively reading from disk.

Let me check if there's a child process of the EngineCore that's doing the actual loading, or if there's some I/O issue.

Actually, wait - vLLM v0.20.2 uses multiprocess architecture. The EngineCore (pid=235762) is a child of 235321. But the `dtype="auto"` might be causing an issue. Let me look at this more carefully.


The model is FP8 quantized with bfloat16 dtype, so it needs to load roughly 32GB of safetensors files from disk. That's a lot of data, and the autodl-tmp storage might just be slow. But after 5+ minutes and only reaching 669 MiB, something feels off - either there's a bottleneck I'm not seeing or the logs might have errors I'm missing. Let me check the latest state of the nohup log to see what's actually happening.

Actually, I realize the issue: when the second test truncated the log file with `>`, it created a new file descriptor, but the orphaned 236811 process still had the old one open and kept writing to it. Both the old and new EngineCore processes ended up writing to the same file, likely with their output interleaving. Rather than keep digging into this, let me just wait and see if the actual pilot test runs successfully.

python3 src/run_fewrel_pilot.py --help\\n    python3 src/run_fewrel_pilot.py --dry-run\\n    python3 src/run_fewrel_pilot.py --model_path /root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen3-32B-FP8\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport Path(\\\"/root/schema-dag-extraction\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef compute_relation_embeddings_st(pid2name: dict) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute relation embeddings using sentence-transformers (CPU).\\n\\n    Args:\\n        pid2name: {pid: relation_name} from fewrel_relation_types.json.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        {relation_name: tensor} embeddings.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer\\n    import torch\\n\\n    local_path =  names = []\\n    texts = []\\n    for pid, name in pid2name.items():\\n        names.append(name)\\n        texts.append(f\\\"Relation type: {name}\\\")\\n\\n    embeddings_np = model.encode(texts, show_progress_bar=False)\\n    return {name: torch.tensor(emb) for name, emb in zip(names, embeddings_np)}\\n\\n\\ndef build_batch_config(sim_matrix, type_names, type_to_idx, counts,\\n   from compute_bcs import compute_bcs\\n    from select_type_batches import (select_high_bcs, select_low_bcs,\\n                                     select_random_batches)\\n\\n    batch_config = {}\\n    for count in counts:\\n        effective = min(count, len(type_names))\\n        low = select_low_bcs(effective, sim_matrix, type_names, type_to_idx)\\n        high = select_high_bcs(effective, sim_matrix, type_names, type_to_idx)\\n        rand = select_random_batches(effective, num_random, type_names,\\n                                     sim_matrix, type_to_idx, seed + count)\\n        batch_config[f\\\"count_{effective}\\\"] = {\\n            \\\"low_bcs\\\": {\\\"types\\\": low, \\\"bcs\\\": compute_bcs(low, sim_matrix, type_to_idx)},\\n            \\\"high_bcs\\\": {\\\"types\\\": high, \\\"bcs\\\": compute_bcs(high, sim_matrix, type_to_idx)},\\n            \\\"random\\\": rand,\\n        }\\n    return batch_config\\n\\n\\ndef run_re_extraction(model_path, samples, batch_config, relation_defs,\\n                      max_model_len=4096):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run RE extraction using vLLM with constrained JSON decoding.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    import sys\\n    sys.path.insert(0, str(BASE_DIR / \\\"src\\\"))\\n    from run_extraction import build_json_schema_re, build_prompt_v2_re\\n\\n    from vllm import LLM, SamplingParams\\n    from vllm.sampling_params import StructuredOutputsParams\\n\\n    llm = LLM(model=model_path, max_model_len=max_model_len,\\n              trust_remote_code=True, dtype=\\\"bfloat16\\\", gpu_memory_utilization=0.95, enforce_eager=True, disable_log_stats=True)\\n\\n    all_results = []\\n\\n    for count_key, batches in batch_config.items():\\n        for batch_label, batch_data in batches.items():\\n            if batch_label == \\\"random\\\":\\n                batch_list = batch_data\\n            else:\\n                batch_list = [batch_data]\\n\\n            for bi, entry in enumerate(batch_list):\\n                batch_types = entry[\\\"types\\\"]\\n                batch_bcs = entry[\\\"bcs\\\"]\\n                bid = (f\\\"{count_key}_{batch_label}_{bi}\\\"\\n                       if batch_label == \\\"random\\\"\\n                       else f\\\"{count_key}_{batch_label}\\\")\\n\\n                batch_samples = [s for s in samples\\n                                 if s[\\\"relation_type\\\"] in batch_types]\\n                if not batch_samples:\\n                    logger.warning(\\\"No samples for batch %s\\\", bid)\\n                    continue\\n\\n                logger.info(\\\"Batch %s: BCS=%.4f, %d types, %d samples\\\",\\n                            bid, batch_bcs, len(batch_types), len(batch_samples))\\n\\n                schema = build_json_schema_re(batch_types)\\n                structured = StructuredOutputsParams(json=schema)\\n                params = SamplingParams(max_tokens=128, temperature=0.0,\\n                                        structured_outputs=structured)\\n\\n                prompts = [\\n                    build_prompt_v2_re(s[\\\"text\\\"], s[\\\"head_entity\\\"],\\n                                      s[\\\"tail_entity\\\"], relation_defs,\\n                                      batch_types)\\n                    for s in batch_samples\\n                ]\\n\\n                t0 = time.time()\\n                outputs = llm.generate(prompts, params)\\n                elapsed = time.time() - t0\\n\\n                for oi, output in enumerate(outputs):\\n                    gen_text = output.outputs[0].text\\n                    try:\\n                        pred = json.loads(gen_text)\\n                    except json.JSONDecodeError:\\n                        pred = {\\\"relation\\\": \\\"PARSE_ERROR\\\"}\\n\\n                    all_results.append({\\n                        \\\"batch_id\\\": bid,\\n                        \\\"count_key\\\": count_key,\\n                        \\\"batch_types\\\": batch_types,\\n                        \\\"batch_bcs\\\": batch_bcs,\\n                        \\\"ground_truth\\\": batch_samples[oi][\\\"relation_type\\\"],\\n                        \\\"prediction\\\": pred.get(\\\"relation\\\", \\\"MISSING\\\"),\\n                        \\\"head_entity\\\": batch_samples[oi][\\\"head_entity\\\"],\\n                        \\\"tail_entity\\\": batch_samples[oi][\\\"tail_entity\\\"],\\n                        \\\"text\\\": batch_samples[oi][\\\"text\\\"][:200],\\n                        \\\"prompt_tokens\\\": len(output.prompt_token_ids),\\n                        \\\"gen_tokens\\\": len(output.outputs[0].token_ids),\\n                        \\\"latency_s\\\": elapsed / len(prompts),\\n                    })\\n\\n                logger.info(\\\"  Done: %.1fs total\\\", elapsed)\\n\\n    return all_results\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_re(results):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute per-batch accuracy.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    stats = defaultdict(lambda: {\\\"correct\\\": 0, \\\"total\\\": len(r[\\\"batch_types\\\"])\\n        if r[\\\"prediction\\\"] == r[\\\"ground_truth\\\"]:\\n            stats[bid][\\\"correct\\\"] += 1\\n\\n    return {\\n        bid: {\\n }\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\\n        description=\\\"FewRel RE Pilot Experiment (D007)\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--model_path\\\",\\n <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Same issue as before - EngineCore (237993) has only read 3.5MB, 669 MiB GPU, no weight loading happening. But the test_vllm.py showed the same PID pattern and eventually loaded fine.

Wait, the test worked. But with the pilot, the EngineCore seems stuck at 669 MiB repeatedly.

Let me check the main Python process and the EngineCore more carefully. Maybe the issue is that in the pilot script, vLLM is being imported after sentence-transformers and torch have already been loaded, and there's some CUDA context conflict.
 
Actually, the test_vllm.py test loaded fine too. In the test, EngineCore progressed from parallel_state initialization to starting the model load within seconds, but in the pilot it's been stuck at parallel_state for over 3 minutes. The key difference might be that the main process is running embedding computation with sentence-transformers, which also loads torch. Even though the embeddings are computed on CPU, this could still create a CUDA fork conflict where the child process deadlocks during CUDA initialization.

This is a known issue with multiprocessing and CUDA—if the parent process has CUDA initialized before vLLM spawns the EngineCore, it can cause a deadlock. The solution would be to either load vLLM first before any torch operations, set the multiprocessing start method to spawn, or configure CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES properly. Let me check what the main process is actually doing right now.
```
